Mixed language, cache and history qualification

P10d2b2b2a · 2026-10-12T00:01:06.361Z · v22.12.0 · linux/x64 · AMD Ryzen 7 5800H with Radeon Graphics. 3 alternating fresh processes per implementation and size; compiled-cli. Both archived/current CLI sources build before timing; measured workers and historical children run without a TypeScript source loader. Builds execute only owned Codiluce code and are excluded from indexing times.

Characterization only · superseded before commitment. These are completed observations of source d0de8990cc75e2f38624ad0d87db68ef416feee7642b87954abc0fe5a5a53068. The 18 mixed and six PHP-pressure processes all passed. Final review found that a partial snapshot write followed by denied cleanup could leave an uncharged file. The corrected source reserves the full possible payload before writing and adds a regression; these observations do not qualify that correction. The remaining source-pressure/deep-history/TS-Laravel series were intentionally held. Exact old source, hashes, logs and terminal receipts are preserved locally under .codiluce/validation-php-residency/pre-partial-write-characterization/. Recorded-source reproduction requires that archived source. Current status.

Local cold/warm/history/tree-memory comparison: PASS
Ten-percent limits against the archived baseline implementation on identical authored mixed workloads. Exact full graph digests hold across cold/population/warm, edited dependency replay versus uncached source, every baseline/current run, and all 4 stored historical snapshots. Every first repetition also compares two history jobs with an independent single-job run. History resume skips all 4 completed snapshots.

The monorepo combines all eighteen Tree-sitter structural adapters, PHP, TypeScript and original Vue/Svelte/Astro sources; Express/Nest, FastAPI/Flask/Django, Rails/Laravel, eight Go router profiles, Java/Kotlin MVC/WebFlux and ASP.NET 8/9/10, and six Rust router/version profiles. Recorded target inputs qualify source contracts on Linux; they do not establish native Windows/macOS execution or framework runtime validity. Target dependencies, toolchains, applications, plugins and hooks never run. The literal fixtures are authored qualification inputs, not upstream repository accuracy evidence.

Cold scans have no cache. Population, warm and a shared Node dependency edit are timed separately. Both original consumer call targets bind to the shared source; the edit invalidates their connected cache unit while unrelated TypeScript units stay hits. Warm graph, syntax and semantic cache events are recorded by analyzer; a qualified PHP graph hit skips native-tree hydration. The cache keeps at most two versions per unit, 256 MiB compressed overall and 64 MiB decoded per entry; oversized/corrupt entries fall back to source rather than blocking indexing.

The 4-commit history edits the shared dependency and an original PHP body, then renames a Vue component; deeper revisions also edit and repeatedly rename an original Python file. Two history workers materialize original Git trees; snapshots include complete entities, relations and diagnostics, not just counts. Only working-tree Git metrics (including absence after removing all Git-only fields) and Git-availability diagnostics are normalized for live-versus-revision comparisons. All semantic metadata, evidence and original ranges remain in the digest.

A separate process polls every thread's descendant list every 25 ms, with an acknowledged start marker before synchronous work. RSS sums include history and parser children; shared pages can be counted repeatedly, exiting processes can make a poll incomplete, and peaks between polls can be missed. Raw parent VmHWM is cumulative since exec, not an isolated warm/history peak. Source memos are limited to 32 MiB of conservative UTF-16 text/key payload and 4,096 entries per instance; compiler/semantic consumers can retain their own text and ASTs, so this is not an RSS cap. Each historical worker now waits until the parent stores or records failure for its one graph before analyzing another commit. The ordinary sample remains below the byte limit; a separate source-pressure profile exercises eviction. Indexed native jobs reserve at most six primary/auxiliary grammars or 16 MiB cumulative original UTF-8 source per generation, then await its exit before a successor. One allowed larger original remains analyzable. Dispatch groups template-host and C-family grammars, then remaining grammars and original paths, so different applications reuse loaded grammars without changing per-file identities. Ordinary originals and their memos are released immediately after declaration consumption; compact deferred template jobs also release their original memo before the next native job. Deferred scripts reread and validate original bytes after the native phase, then release that memo after consumption. Indexing awaits PHP parser retirement too, so no parser child survives completion into warm replay or the next historical revision. Once all declarations and deferred scripts are consumed and parser retirement completes, the shared source memo is cleared; subsequent consumers validate any needed rereads through the indexed boundary. Main-process per-original release counts, phase retirement counts and released payloads are recorded below. The superseded retained-source characterization preserves all 60 completed observations, including its failed warm-pressure memory guard. Phase-end clearing alone also failed; its full sixty-process record and preceding narrowly passing characterization remain available. Native observations cover main-process indexing only; history children follow the same policy. These thresholds bound lifetimes, not native allocation or RSS. Per-commit RSS checkpoints are observations after result delivery, not instantaneous or independent peaks. Native OS readers, broader AST/consumer budgets, much longer production histories and production-repository accuracy remain additional P10d gates. PHP provider-owned residency accounts twice the original native serialized AST payload plus UTF-16 source/key text, capped at 32 MiB or 4,096 units. Evicted units use checksum-verified private ephemeral snapshots, limited to 256 MiB or 4,096 entries; unavailable snapshots reparse validated originals. Six source reads/native jobs share a 16 MiB original-input reservation, with one allowed larger original admitted alone. Consumer-owned AST/semantic references remain separate; these are representation/ownership limits, not a decoded-object or RSS cap. Main-process PHP counters are recorded; historical children use the same policy. This report records its named phase against the archived baseline.
